ol>li:first-child::before{content:" ("counter(listitem)"\a0 "}</style><div class="hlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:SJ" title="ISO 3166-2:SJ">SJ</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:NO" TLD</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"/><div class="hlist"><ul><li><a href="/wiki/.no" title=".no">.no</a></li><li><a href="/wiki/.sj" title=".sj">.sj</a><sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Svalbard</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;s&#39; in &#39;sigh&#39;">s</span><span title="&#39;v&#39; in &#39;vie&#39;">v</span><span title="/ɑː/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;father&#39;">ɑː</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ɑːr/: &#39;ar&#39; in &#39;far&#39;">ɑːr</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">SVAHL</span>-bar</i></a>,<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> <small>Urban East Norwegian:&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="no-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Norwegian" title="Help:IPA/Norwegian">[ˈsvɑ̂ːɫbɑr]</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:Svalbard_audio.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/b/b8/Svalbard_audio.ogg" class="internal" title="Svalbard audio.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span>), also known as <b>Spitsbergen</b>, or <b>Spitzbergen</b>, is a <a href="/wiki/Norway" title="Norway"> Norwegian</a> <a href="/wiki/Archipelago" title="Archipelago">archipelago</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Arctic_Ocean" title="Arctic Ocean">Arctic Ocean</a>. North of <a href="/wiki/Continental_Europe" title="Continental Europe">mainland Europe</a>, it lies about midway between the northern coast of Norway and the <a href="/wiki/North_Pole" title="North Pole">North Pole</a>. The islands of the group range from <a href="/wiki/74th_parallel_north" title="74th parallel north">74°</a> to <a href="/wiki/81st_parallel_north" title="81st parallel north">81° north</a> latitude, and from <a href="/wiki/10th_meridian_east" title="10th meridian east">10°</a> to <a href="/wiki/35th_meridian_east" title="35th meridian east">35° east</a> longitude. The largest island is <a href="/wiki/Spitsbergen" title="Spitsbergen">Spitsbergen</a>, followed in size by <a href="/wiki/Nordaustlandet" title="Nordaustlandet">Nordaustlandet</a> and <span title="Norwegian-language text"><span lang="no" style="font-style: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Edge%C3%B8ya" title="Edgeøya">Edgeøya</a></span></span><span class="lang-comment" style="font-style: normal; display: none; color: #33aa33; margin-left: 0.3em;">code: nor promoted to code: no </span>. The largest settlement is <a href="/wiki/Longyearbyen" title="Longyearbyen">Longyearbyen</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-SA-2021_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SA-2021-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Whaling" title="Whaling"> Whalers</a> who sailed far north in the 17th and 18th centuries used the islands as a base; subsequently the archipelago was abandoned. <a href="/wiki/Coal_mining" title="Coal mining">Coal mining</a> started at the beginning of the 20th century, and several permanent communities were established. The <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Treaty" title="Svalbard Treaty">Svalbard Treaty</a> of 1920 recognizes Norwegian sovereignty, and the Norwegian <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Act" title="Svalbard Act">Svalbard Act</a> of 1925 made Svalbard a full part of the Kingdom of Norway. The Svalbard Treaty established Svalbard as a <a href="/wiki/Free_economic_zone" title="Free economic zone">free economic zone</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Demilitarized_zone" title="Demilitarized zone">demilitarized zone</a>. The Norwegian <a href="/wiki/Store_Norske_Spitsbergen_Kulkompani" title="Store Norske Spitsbergen Kulkompani">Store Norske</a> and the Russian <span title="Russian-language text"><i lang="ru-Latn"><a href="/wiki/Arktikugol" title="Arktikugol">Arktikugol</a></i></span><span class="lang-comment" style="font-style: normal; display: none; color: #33aa33; margin-left: 0.3em;">code: rus promoted to code: ru </span> remain the only mining companies in place. Research and tourism have become important supplementary industries, with the <a href="/wiki/University_Centre_in_Svalbard" title="University Centre in Svalbard">University Centre in Svalbard</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Global_Seed_Vault" title="Svalbard Global Seed Vault">Svalbard Global Seed Vault</a> playing critical roles in the local economy. Apart from Longyearbyen, other settlements include the Russian mining-community of <a href="/wiki/Barentsburg" title="Barentsburg">Barentsburg</a>, the Norwegian research-station of <a href="/wiki/Ny-%C3%85lesund" title="Ny-Ålesund">Ny-Ålesund</a>, and the Swedish-Norwegian mining outpost of <a href="/wiki/Sveagruva" title="Sveagruva">Sveagruva</a> (which closed in 2020). Other settlements lie farther north, but are populated only by rotating groups of researchers. No roads connect the settlements; instead <a href="/wiki/Snowmobile" title="Snowmobile">snowmobiles</a>, aircraft and boats provide inter-settlement transport. <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Airport" title="Svalbard Airport">Svalbard Airport</a> serves as the main gateway.
</p><p>Approximately 60% of the archipelago is covered with <a href="/wiki/Glacier" title="Glacier">glaciers</a>, and the islands feature many mountains and <a href="/wiki/Fjord" title="Fjord">fjords</a>. The archipelago has an <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_the_Arctic" title="Climate of the Arctic"> Arctic climate</a>, although with significantly higher temperatures than other areas at the same latitude. The <a href="/wiki/Flora_of_Svalbard" title="Flora of Svalbard">flora</a> has adapted to take advantage of the long period of <a href="/wiki/Midnight_sun" title="Midnight sun">midnight sun</a> to compensate for the <a href="/wiki/Polar_night" title="Polar night">polar night</a>. Many <a href="/wiki/Seabird" title="Seabird">seabirds</a> use Svalbard as a breeding ground, and it is home to <a href="/wiki/Polar_bear" title="Polar bear">polar bears</a>, <a href="/wiki/Reindeer" title="Reindeer">reindeer</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Arctic_fox" title="Arctic fox">Arctic fox</a>, and certain marine mammals. <a href="/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_Norway" title="List of national parks of Norway">Seven national parks</a> and 23 nature-reserves cover two-thirds of the archipelago, protecting the largely untouched fragile environment.
</p><p>While part of the Kingdom of Norway since 1925, Svalbard is not part of geographical Norway; administratively, the archipelago is not part of any <a href="/wiki/Counties_of_Norway" title="Counties of Norway"> Norwegian county</a>, but forms an <a href="/wiki/Unincorporated_area" title="Unincorporated area">unincorporated area</a>. This means that it is administered directly by the Norwegian government through an appointed <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Svalbard" title="Governor of Svalbard"> governor</a>, and is a special jurisdiction subject to the Svalbard Treaty that is outside of the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Area" title="Schengen Area">Schengen Area</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Nordic_Passport_Union" title="Nordic Passport Union">Nordic Passport Union</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/European_Economic_Area" title="European Economic Area">European Economic Area</a>. <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_and_Jan_Mayen" title="Svalbard and Jan Mayen">Svalbard and Jan Mayen</a> are collectively assigned the <a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-1_alpha-2" title="ISO 3166-1 alpha-2">ISO 3166-1 alpha-2</a> country-code "SJ". Both areas are administered by Norway, though they are separated by a distance of over 950 kilometres (510 nautical miles) and have very different administrative structures.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Names">Names</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Names">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Svalbard_oli_2020236_detail.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Svalbard_oli_2020236_detail.jpg/220px-Svalbard_oli_2020236_detail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Svalbard_oli_2020236_detail.jpg/330px-Svalbard_oli_2020236_detail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/Svalbard_oli_2020236_detail.jpg/440px-Svalbard_oli_2020236_detail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="720" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Svalbard_oli_2020236_detail.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Late-summer satellite view of Spitsbergen, showing the Holmstrom Glacier and meltwater pools stained deep-red by glacial silt, from erosion of soft, iron-rich <a href="/wiki/Devonian" title="Devonian">Devonian</a> sediments</div></div></div>
<p>The name <i>Svalbard</i> was officially adopted for the archipelago by Norway under the 1925 <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Act" title="Svalbard Act">Svalbard Act</a> which formally annexed it.<sup id="cite_ref-Berg2013_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Berg2013-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The former name <i>Spitsbergen</i> was thenceforth restricted to the main island. In 1827 <a href="/wiki/Baltazar_Keilhau" class="mw-redirect" title="Baltazar Keilhau">Baltazar Keilhau</a> first proposed that the <a href="/wiki/Old_Norse" title="Old Norse">Old Norse</a> toponym <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">Svalbarði</i></span>, found in medieval Icelandic sources, referred to Spitsbergen.<sup id="cite_ref-Berg2013_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Berg2013-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Isachsen1907_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Isachsen1907-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Keilhau's theory was revived by <a href="/wiki/Gustav_Storm" title="Gustav Storm">Gustav Storm</a> in 1890 and <a href="/wiki/Gunnar_Isachsen" title="Gunnar Isachsen">Gunnar Isachsen</a> in 1907, at a time when ancient Norse connection to the land would help modern Norway's contested claim to sovereignty.<sup id="cite_ref-Berg2013_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Berg2013-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Isachsen1907_8-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Isachsen1907-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> <span title="Norwegian-language text"><i lang="no">Svalbard</i></span> is a <a href="/wiki/Modern_Norwegian" title="Modern Norwegian">modern Norwegian</a> analogue of <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">Svalbarði</i></span>, which in turn derives from <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">svalr</i></span> ('cold') and <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">barð</i></span> ('edge', 'ridge', 'turf', 'beard').<sup id="cite_ref-Isachsen1907_8-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Isachsen1907-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Icelandic_Annals" title="Icelandic Annals">Icelandic Annals</a> record that <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">Svalbarði</i></span> was discovered in 1194, while the <i><a href="/wiki/Landn%C3%A1mab%C3%B3k" title="Landnámabók">Landnámabók</a></i> places it four <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">dægr</i></span>s' sailing north of <a href="/wiki/Langanes" title="Langanes">Langanes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Isachsen1907_8-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Isachsen1907-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The word <i lang="non"><a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/dagr#Old_Norse" class="extiw" title="wikt:dagr">dægr</a></i> "day" might mean either 12 or 24 hours; Isachsen took the latter interpretation, thus discounting <a href="/wiki/Jan_Mayen" title="Jan Mayen">Jan Mayen</a> as <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">Svalbarði</i></span>.<sup id="cite_ref-Isachsen1907_8-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Isachsen1907-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cultural_studies" title="Cultural studies">Cultural studies</a> academic Roald Berg says <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">Svalbarði</i></span> more likely referred to part of <a href="/wiki/Greenland" title="Greenland">Greenland</a>, and the 1925 renaming cemented Norwegian sovereignty as recognised by the 1920 Spitsbergen Treaty (now the <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Treaty" title="Svalbard Treaty">Svalbard Treaty</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-Berg2013_7-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Berg2013-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The name <i>Spitsbergen</i> originated with Dutch navigator and explorer <a href="/wiki/Willem_Barentsz" title="Willem Barentsz">Willem Barentsz</a>, who in 1596 described the "pointed mountains" or, in Dutch, <i>spitse bergen</i> that he saw on the west coast of the main island. Barentsz did not recognize that he had discovered an archipelago, and consequently the name <i>Spitsbergen</i> long remained in use both for the main island and for the archipelago as a whole.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Later the main island was sometimes distinguished as <i>West Spitsbergen</i>. The spelling <span title="German-language text"><i lang="de">Spitzbergen</i></span>, with z instead of s, derives from German.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Svalbard" title="Geography of Svalbard">Geography of Svalbard</a></div>
<p>The Svalbard Treaty of 1920<sup id="cite_ref-cia_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> defines Svalbard as all islands, islets, and <a href="/wiki/Skerry" title="Skerry">skerries</a> from 74° to 81° north latitude, and from 10° to 35° east longitude.<sup id="cite_ref-npi_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-npi-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-treaty_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-treaty-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The land area is 61,022&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (23,561&#160;sq&#160;mi), and dominated by the island of Spitsbergen, which constitutes more than half the archipelago, followed by Nordaustlandet and Edgeøya.<sup id="cite_ref-islandsize_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-islandsize-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> All settlements are on Spitsbergen, except the meteorological outposts on <a href="/wiki/Bear_Island_(Norway)" title="Bear Island (Norway)">Bjørnøya</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hopen,_Svalbard" class="mw-redirect" title="Hopen, Svalbard">Hopen</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The Norwegian state took possession of all unclaimed land, or 95.2% of the archipelago, at the time the Svalbard Treaty entered into force; <a href="/wiki/Store_Norske_Spitsbergen_Kulkompani" title="Store Norske Spitsbergen Kulkompani">Store Norske</a>, a Norwegian coal mining company, owns 4%, <a href="/wiki/Arktikugol" title="Arktikugol">Arktikugol</a>, a Russian coal mining company, owns 0.4%, while other private owners hold 0.4%.<sup id="cite_ref-fisheries_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fisheries-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As Svalbard is north of the <a href="/wiki/Arctic_Circle" title="Arctic Circle">Arctic Circle</a>, it experiences <a href="/wiki/Midnight_sun" title="Midnight sun">midnight sun</a> in summer and <a href="/wiki/Polar_night" title="Polar night">polar night</a> in winter. At 74° north, the midnight sun lasts 99 days and polar night 84 days, while the respective figures at 81° north are 141 and 128 days.<sup id="cite_ref-sun_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sun-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> In Longyearbyen, midnight sun lasts from 20 April until 23 August, and polar night lasts from 26 October to 15 February.<sup id="cite_ref-npi_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-npi-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> In winter, the combination of <a href="/wiki/Full_moon" title="Full moon">full moon</a> and reflective snow can give additional light.<sup id="cite_ref-sun_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sun-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Due to the Earth's tilt and the high latitude, Svalbard has extensive twilights. Longyearbyen sees the first and last day of polar night having seven and a half hours of twilight, whereas the perpetual light lasts for two weeks longer than the midnight sun.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> On the summer solstice, the sun bottoms out at 12° sun angle in the middle of the night, being much higher during night than in mainland Norway's polar light areas.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> However, the daytime strength of the sun remains as low as 35°.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Glacier" title="Glacier">Glacial ice</a> covers 36,502&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (14,094&#160;sq&#160;mi) or 60% of Svalbard; 30% is barren rock while 10% is vegetated.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> The largest glacier is <a href="/wiki/Austfonna" title="Austfonna">Austfonna</a> (8,412&#160;km<sup>2</sup> or 3,248&#160;sq&#160;mi) on Nordaustlandet, followed by <a href="/wiki/Olav_V_Land" title="Olav V Land">Olav V Land</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vestfonna" title="Vestfonna">Vestfonna</a>. During summer, it is possible to ski from <a href="/wiki/S%C3%B8rkapp" title="Sørkapp">Sørkapp</a> in the south to the north of Spitsbergen, with only a short distance not being covered by snow or glacier. <a href="/wiki/Kvit%C3%B8ya" title="Kvitøya">Kvitøya</a> is 99.3% covered by glacier.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The landforms of Svalbard were created through <a href="/wiki/Quaternary_glaciation" title="Quaternary glaciation">repeated ice ages</a>, when glaciers cut the former plateau into fjords, valleys, and mountains.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> The tallest peak is <a href="/wiki/Newtontoppen" title="Newtontoppen">Newtontoppen</a> (1,717&#160;m or 5,633&#160;ft), followed by <a href="/wiki/Perriertoppen" title="Perriertoppen">Perriertoppen</a> (1,712&#160;m or 5,617&#160;ft), <a href="/wiki/Ceresfjellet" title="Ceresfjellet">Ceresfjellet</a> (1,675&#160;m or 5,495&#160;ft), <a href="/wiki/Chadwickryggen" title="Chadwickryggen">Chadwickryggen</a> (1,640&#160;m or 5,380&#160;ft), and <a href="/wiki/Galileotoppen" title="Galileotoppen">Galileotoppen</a> (1,637&#160;m or 5,371&#160;ft). The longest fjord is <a href="/wiki/Wijdefjorden" title="Wijdefjorden">Wijdefjorden</a> (108&#160;km or 67&#160;mi), followed by <a href="/wiki/Isfjord,_Svalbard" class="mw-redirect" title="Isfjord, Svalbard">Isfjorden</a> (107&#160;km or 66&#160;mi), <a href="/wiki/Van_Mijenfjorden" title="Van Mijenfjorden">Van Mijenfjorden</a> (83&#160;km or 52&#160;mi), <a href="/wiki/Woodfjorden" title="Woodfjorden">Woodfjorden</a> (64&#160;km or 40&#160;mi), and <a href="/wiki/Wahlenbergfjorden" title="Wahlenbergfjorden">Wahlenbergfjorden</a> (46&#160;km or 29&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Svalbard is part of the <a href="/wiki/High_Arctic_Large_Igneous_Province" title="High Arctic Large Igneous Province">High Arctic Large Igneous Province</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> and experienced Norway's strongest earthquake on 6 March 2009 at magnitude 6.5.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Svalbard" title="History of Svalbard">History of Svalbard</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Category:Dutch_exploration_in_the_Age_of_Discovery" title="Category:Dutch exploration in the Age of Discovery">Dutch Republic in the Age of Discovery</a>, <a href="/wiki/Early_modern_Netherlandish_cartography" class="mw-redirect" title="Early modern Netherlandish cartography">Early modern Netherlandish cartography</a>, <a href="/wiki/Willem_Barentsz" title="Willem Barentsz">Willem Barentsz</a>, <a href="/wiki/Northeast_Passage" title="Northeast Passage">Northeast Passage</a>, <a href="/wiki/Arctic_exploration" title="Arctic exploration">Arctic exploration</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Terra_nullius" title="Terra nullius">Terra nullius</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Barentsz_arctic_map.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Barentsz_arctic_map.jpg/220px-Barentsz_arctic_map.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="175" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Barentsz_arctic_map.jpg/330px-Barentsz_arctic_map.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Barentsz_arctic_map.jpg/440px-Barentsz_arctic_map.jpg 2x" data-file-width="650" data-file-height="517" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Barentsz_arctic_map.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Svalbard, here mapped for the first time, is indicated as "Het Nieuwe Land" (Dutch for 'the New Land'), center-left. A portion of a 1599 map of Arctic exploration by <a href="/wiki/Willem_Barentsz" title="Willem Barentsz">Willem Barentsz</a>.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:182px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Arctic.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Arctic.svg/180px-Arctic.svg.png" decoding="async" width="180" height="226" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Arctic.svg/270px-Arctic.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Arctic.svg/360px-Arctic.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="505" data-file-height="633" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Arctic.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>In the <a href="/wiki/Category:Dutch_exploration_in_the_Age_of_Discovery" title="Category:Dutch exploration in the Age of Discovery">Golden Age of Dutch exploration and discovery</a> (c. 1590s–1720s), Dutch navigators were <a href="/wiki/List_of_Dutch_explorations" title="List of Dutch explorations">the first explorers</a> and <a href="/wiki/Early_modern_Netherlandish_cartography" class="mw-redirect" title="Early modern Netherlandish cartography">mapped many largely unknown isolated areas of the world</a>, including the Svalbard archipelago and <a href="/wiki/Jan_Mayen#History" title="Jan Mayen">Jan Mayen</a> in the Arctic Ocean.</div></div></div>
<h3><span id="Dutch_discovery.2C_exploration.2C_and_mapping_of_a_terra_nullius"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Dutch_discovery,_exploration,_and_mapping_of_a_terra_nullius">Dutch discovery, exploration, and mapping of a <i>terra nullius</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Dutch discovery, exploration, and mapping of a terra nullius">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Dutch_mapping_of_Svalbard" class="mw-redirect" title="Dutch mapping of Svalbard">Dutch mapping of Svalbard</a></div>
<p>The Dutchman <a href="/wiki/Willem_Barentsz" title="Willem Barentsz">Willem Barentsz</a> made the first discovery of the archipelago in 1596, when he sighted the coast of the island of Spitsbergen while searching for the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Sea_Route" title="Northern Sea Route">Northern Sea Route</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The first recorded landing on the islands of Svalbard dates to 1604, when an English ship landed at <i>Bjørnøya</i>, or Bear Island, and started hunting <a href="/wiki/Walrus" title="Walrus">walrus</a>. Annual expeditions soon followed, and Spitsbergen became a base for hunting the <a href="/wiki/Bowhead_whale" title="Bowhead whale">bowhead whale</a> from 1611.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Because of the lawless nature of the area, <a href="/wiki/England" title="England">English</a>, <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Danish</a>, <a href="/wiki/Netherlands" title="Netherlands">Dutch</a>, and <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">French</a> companies and authorities tried to use force to keep out other countries' fleets.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="17th.E2.80.9318th_centuries"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="17th–18th_centuries">17th–18th centuries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: 17th–18th centuries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:282px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Traankokerijen_bij_het_dorp_Smerenburg_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-2355.jpeg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Traankokerijen_bij_het_dorp_Smerenburg_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-2355.jpeg/280px-Traankokerijen_bij_het_dorp_Smerenburg_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-2355.jpeg" decoding="async" width="280" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Traankokerijen_bij_het_dorp_Smerenburg_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-2355.jpeg/420px-Traankokerijen_bij_het_dorp_Smerenburg_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-2355.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Traankokerijen_bij_het_dorp_Smerenburg_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-2355.jpeg/560px-Traankokerijen_bij_het_dorp_Smerenburg_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-2355.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="6268" data-file-height="3200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Traankokerijen_bij_het_dorp_Smerenburg_Rijksmuseum_SK-A-2355.jpeg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The whaling station of the Amsterdam chamber of the Northern Company in <a href="/wiki/Smeerenburg" title="Smeerenburg">Smeerenburg</a>, by <a href="/wiki/Cornelis_de_Man" title="Cornelis de Man">Cornelis de Man</a> (1639), but based on a painting of a <i>Dansk hvalfangststation</i> (Danish whaling station) by A.B.R. Speeck (1634), which represented the Danish station in Copenhagen Bay (Kobbefjorden)</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Smeerenburg" title="Smeerenburg">Smeerenburg</a> was one of the first settlements, established by the Dutch in 1619.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Smaller bases were also built by the English, Danish, and French. At first the outposts were merely summer camps, but from the early 1630s, a few individuals started to <a href="/wiki/Overwintering" title="Overwintering">overwinter</a>. Whaling at Spitsbergen lasted until the 1820s, when the Dutch, British, and Danish whalers moved elsewhere in the Arctic.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> By the late 17th century, <a href="/wiki/Russians" title="Russians">Russian</a> hunters arrived; they overwintered to a greater extent and hunted land mammals such as the polar bear and fox.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="19th_century">19th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: 19th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Norwegian hunting—mostly for walrus—started in the 1790s. The first Norwegian citizens to reach Spitsbergen proper were a number of Coast <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A1mi" title="Sámi">Sámi people</a> from the <a href="/wiki/Hammerfest" title="Hammerfest">Hammerfest</a> region, who were hired as part of a Russian crew for an expedition in 1795.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Russian_War_(1807%E2%80%931812)" title="Anglo-Russian War (1807–1812)">Anglo-Russian War</a> in 1809, Russian activity on Svalbard diminished, and ceased by the 1820s.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Norwegian whaling was abandoned about the same time as the Russians left,<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> but whaling continued around Spitsbergen until the 1830s, and around <a href="/wiki/Bear_Island_(Norway)" title="Bear Island (Norway)">Bjørnøya</a> until the 1860s.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_century">20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Svalbard_Treaty_and_Norwegian_sovereignty">Svalbard Treaty and Norwegian sovereignty</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Svalbard Treaty and Norwegian sovereignty">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Treaty" title="Svalbard Treaty">Svalbard Treaty</a></div>
<p>By the 1890s, Svalbard had become a destination for Arctic tourism, coal deposits had been found, and the islands were being used as a base for <a href="/wiki/Arctic_exploration" title="Arctic exploration">Arctic exploration</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> The first mining was along Isfjorden by Norwegians in 1899; by 1904, British interests had established themselves in <a href="/wiki/Adventfjorden" title="Adventfjorden">Adventfjorden</a> and started the first year-round operations.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> Production in Longyearbyen, by US interests, started in 1908;<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> and Store Norske established itself in 1916, as did other Norwegian interests during the first world war, in part by buying US interests.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Proposed_flag_of_Svalbard,_Norway_(1930).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Proposed_flag_of_Svalbard%2C_Norway_%281930%29.svg/220px-Proposed_flag_of_Svalbard%2C_Norway_%281930%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Proposed_flag_of_Svalbard%2C_Norway_%281930%29.svg/330px-Proposed_flag_of_Svalbard%2C_Norway_%281930%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Proposed_flag_of_Svalbard%2C_Norway_%281930%29.svg/440px-Proposed_flag_of_Svalbard%2C_Norway_%281930%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Proposed_flag_of_Svalbard,_Norway_(1930).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proposed flag of Svalbard from 1930.<sup id="cite_ref-Jan_Oskar_Engene_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jan_Oskar_Engene-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> It consists of the lion from the <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Norway" title="Coat of arms of Norway">Norwegian coat of arms</a> on a background of blue and white <a href="/wiki/Vair" title="Vair">vair</a>, possibly symbolising the Arctic landscape of the archipelago. Svalbard does not currently use a distinct flag.</div></div></div>
<p>Discussions to establish the sovereignty of the archipelago commenced in the 1910s,<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> but were interrupted by <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> On 9 February 1920, following the <a href="/wiki/Paris_Peace_Conference,_1919" class="mw-redirect" title="Paris Peace Conference, 1919">Paris Peace Conference</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Treaty" title="Svalbard Treaty">Svalbard Treaty</a> was signed, granting full sovereignty to Norway. However, all signatory countries were granted non-discriminatory rights to fishing, hunting, and mineral resources.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> The treaty took effect on 14 August 1925, at the same time as the <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Act" title="Svalbard Act">Svalbard Act</a> regulated the archipelago and the first <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Svalbard" title="Governor of Svalbard">governor</a>, <a href="/wiki/Johannes_Gerckens_Bass%C3%B8e" title="Johannes Gerckens Bassøe">Johannes Gerckens Bassøe</a>, took office.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The archipelago has traditionally been known as Spitsbergen, and the main island as West Spitsbergen. From the 1920s, Norway renamed the archipelago Svalbard, and the main island became Spitsbergen.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Kvit%C3%B8ya" title="Kvitøya">Kvitøya</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kong_Karls_Land" title="Kong Karls Land">Kong Karls Land</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hopen_(Svalbard)" title="Hopen (Svalbard)">Hopen</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Bear_Island_(Norway)" title="Bear Island (Norway)">Bjørnøya</a> were not regarded as part of the Spitsbergen archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Russians have traditionally called the archipelago <i>Grumant</i> (<span title="Russian-language text"><span lang="ru">Грумант</span></span>).<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> retained the name Spitsbergen (<span title="Russian-language text"><span lang="ru">Шпицберген</span></span>) to support undocumented claims that Russians were the first to discover the island.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1928, Italian explorer <a href="/wiki/Umberto_Nobile" title="Umberto Nobile">Umberto Nobile</a> and the crew of the airship <i>Italia</i> crashed on the icepack off the coast of <a href="/wiki/Foyn%C3%B8ya" title="Foynøya">Foyn Island</a>. The subsequent rescue attempts were covered extensively in the press and Svalbard received short-lived fame as a result.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Second_World_War">Second World War</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Second World War">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Demolition_of_wireless_station_at_Spitzbergen,_Operation_Gauntlet,_1941_(22418716705).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Demolition_of_wireless_station_at_Spitzbergen%2C_Operation_Gauntlet%2C_1941_%2822418716705%29.jpg/220px-Demolition_of_wireless_station_at_Spitzbergen%2C_Operation_Gauntlet%2C_1941_%2822418716705%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Demolition_of_wireless_station_at_Spitzbergen%2C_Operation_Gauntlet%2C_1941_%2822418716705%29.jpg/330px-Demolition_of_wireless_station_at_Spitzbergen%2C_Operation_Gauntlet%2C_1941_%2822418716705%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6a/Demolition_of_wireless_station_at_Spitzbergen%2C_Operation_Gauntlet%2C_1941_%2822418716705%29.jpg/440px-Demolition_of_wireless_station_at_Spitzbergen%2C_Operation_Gauntlet%2C_1941_%2822418716705%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2602" data-file-height="1765" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Demolition_of_wireless_station_at_Spitzbergen,_Operation_Gauntlet,_1941_(22418716705).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Demolition of the wireless station during <a href="/wiki/Operation_Gauntlet" title="Operation Gauntlet">Operation Gauntlet</a> in 1941</div></div></div>
<p>Svalbard, known to both British and Germans as Spitsbergen, was little affected by the <a href="/wiki/German_invasion_of_Norway" class="mw-redirect" title="German invasion of Norway">German invasion of Norway</a> in April 1940. The settlements continued to operate as before, mining coal and monitoring the weather.
</p><p>In July 1941, following the German invasion of the Soviet Union, the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> reconnoitered the islands with a view to using them as a base of operations to send supplies to north Russia, but the idea was rejected as impractical.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Instead, with the agreement of the Soviets and the Norwegian government in exile, in August 1941 the Norwegian and Soviet settlements on Svalbard were evacuated, and facilities there destroyed, in <a href="/wiki/Operation_Gauntlet" title="Operation Gauntlet">Operation Gauntlet</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
However the Norwegian government in exile decided it would be important politically to establish a garrison in the islands, which was done in May 1942 during <a href="/wiki/Operation_Fritham" title="Operation Fritham">Operation Fritham</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Meanwhile, the Germans responded to the destruction of the weather station by establishing a reporting station of their own, codenamed <a href="/wiki/Operation_Bans%C3%B6" class="mw-redirect" title="Operation Bansö">"Banso"</a>, in October 1941.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> This was chased away in November by a visit from four British warships, but later returned. A second station, "Knospel", was established at <a href="/wiki/Ny-%C3%85lesund" title="Ny-Ålesund">Ny-Ålesund</a> in 1941, remaining until 1942. In May 1942, after the arrival of the Fritham force, the German unit at Banso was evacuated.
</p><p>In September 1943 in <a href="/wiki/Operation_Zitronella" title="Operation Zitronella">Operation Zitronella</a> a German task force, which included the battleship <a href="/wiki/German_battleship_Tirpitz" title="German battleship Tirpitz"><i>Tirpitz</i></a>, was sent to attack the garrison and destroy the settlements at Longyearbyen and Barentsburg.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> This was achieved, but had little long-term effect: after their departure the Norwegians returned and re-established their presence.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 1944, the Germans set up their last weather station, <a href="/wiki/Operation_Haudegen" title="Operation Haudegen">Operation Haudegen</a> in NordOstLand; it functioned until after the German surrender. On 4 September 1945, the soldiers were picked up by a Norwegian seal hunting vessel and surrendered to its captain. This group of men were the last German troops to surrender after the Second World War.
</p><p>After the war, the Soviet Union proposed common Norwegian and Soviet administration and military defence of Svalbard. This was rejected in 1947 by Norway, which two years later joined <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>. The Soviet Union retained high civilian activity on Svalbard, in part to ensure that the archipelago was not used by NATO.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Post-war">Post-<span lang="en" dir="ltr">war</span></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Post-war">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kulltaubane.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Kulltaubane.jpg/220px-Kulltaubane.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Kulltaubane.jpg/330px-Kulltaubane.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Kulltaubane.jpg/440px-Kulltaubane.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="366" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kulltaubane.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Abandoned <a href="/wiki/Aerial_tramway" title="Aerial tramway">aerial tramway</a> previously used for transporting coal</div></div></div>
<p>After the war, Norway re-established operations at Longyearbyen and Ny-Ålesund,<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> while the Soviet Union established mining in Barentsburg, <a href="/wiki/Pyramiden" title="Pyramiden">Pyramiden</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Grumant" title="Grumant">Grumant</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-t202_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-t202-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The mine at Ny-Ålesund had several fatal accidents, killing 71 people while it was in operation from 1945 to 1954 and from 1960 to 1963. The <a href="/wiki/Kings_Bay_Affair" title="Kings Bay Affair">Kings Bay Affair</a>, caused by the 1962 accident killing 21 workers, forced <a href="/wiki/Gerhardsen%27s_Third_Cabinet" title="Gerhardsen&#39;s Third Cabinet">Gerhardsen's Third Cabinet</a> to resign.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From 1964, Ny-Ålesund became a research outpost, and a facility for the <a href="/wiki/European_Space_Research_Organisation" title="European Space Research Organisation">European Space Research Organisation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Petroleum test drilling was started in 1963 and continued until 1984, but no commercially viable fields were found.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> From 1960, regular charter flights were made from the mainland to a field at <a href="/wiki/Hotellneset" title="Hotellneset">Hotellneset</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> in 1975, Svalbard Airport, Longyearbyen opened, allowing year-round services.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the <a href="/wiki/Cold_War" title="Cold War">Cold War</a>, the Soviet Union comprised about two-thirds of the population on the islands (Norwegians making up the remaining third) with the population of the archipelago slightly under 4,000.<sup id="cite_ref-t202_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-t202-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Russian activity has diminished considerably since then, falling from 2,500 to 450 people from 1990 to 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-demographics_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-demographics-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Grumant was closed after it was depleted in 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-t202_60-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-t202-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Pyramiden was closed in 1998.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Coal exports from Barentsburg ceased in 2006 because of a fire,<sup id="cite_ref-chp10_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chp10-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> but resumed in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The Russians  experienced two air accidents: <a href="/wiki/Vnukovo_Airlines_Flight_2801" title="Vnukovo Airlines Flight 2801">Vnukovo Airlines Flight 2801</a>, which killed 141 people,<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> and the <a href="/wiki/Heerodden_helicopter_accident" class="mw-redirect" title="Heerodden helicopter accident">Heerodden helicopter accident</a>, which killed three people.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Longyearbyen remained purely a company town until 1989 when utilities, culture, and education was separated into Svalbard Samfunnsdrift.<sup id="cite_ref-ah49_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ah49-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> In 1993, it was sold to the national government and the University Centre was established.<sup id="cite_ref-unis_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unis-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Through the 1990s, tourism increased and the town developed an economy independent of Store Norske and mining.<sup id="cite_ref-naering_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-naering-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Longyearbyen was incorporated on 1 January 2002, adopting a community council.<sup id="cite_ref-ah49_74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ah49-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Population">Population</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Population">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:BarentsburgFromDock.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/BarentsburgFromDock.JPG/220px-BarentsburgFromDock.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="166" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/BarentsburgFromDock.JPG/330px-BarentsburgFromDock.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/BarentsburgFromDock.JPG/440px-BarentsburgFromDock.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2816" data-file-height="2120" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:BarentsburgFromDock.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The dock house in <a href="/wiki/Barentsburg" title="Barentsburg">Barentsburg</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Polar_bear_sign_Svalbard.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Polar_bear_sign_Svalbard.jpg/220px-Polar_bear_sign_Svalbard.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Polar_bear_sign_Svalbard.jpg/330px-Polar_bear_sign_Svalbard.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Polar_bear_sign_Svalbard.jpg/440px-Polar_bear_sign_Svalbard.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2496" data-file-height="1664" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Polar_bear_sign_Svalbard.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A sign warning of <a href="/wiki/Polar_bear" title="Polar bear">polar bears</a> on a road in Svalbard</div></div></div>
<p>In 2016, Svalbard had a population of 2,667 of which 423 were Russian and Ukrainian, 10 Polish, and 322 non-Norwegians living in Norwegian settlements.<sup id="cite_ref-islandsize_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-islandsize-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The largest non-Norwegian groups in Longyearbyen in 2005 were from Russia, Ukraine, Poland, Germany, Sweden, Denmark, and Thailand.<sup id="cite_ref-demographics_68-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-demographics-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Settlements">Settlements</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Settlements">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Longyearbyen_colourful_homes.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Longyearbyen_colourful_homes.jpg/220px-Longyearbyen_colourful_homes.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Longyearbyen_colourful_homes.jpg/330px-Longyearbyen_colourful_homes.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Longyearbyen_colourful_homes.jpg/440px-Longyearbyen_colourful_homes.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3872" data-file-height="2592" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Longyearbyen_colourful_homes.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Company homes, <a href="/wiki/Longyearbyen" title="Longyearbyen">Longyearbyen</a></div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Longyearbyen" title="Longyearbyen">Longyearbyen</a> is the largest settlement on the archipelago, the seat of the governor and the only incorporated town. The town features a hospital, <a href="/wiki/Longyearbyen_School" title="Longyearbyen School">primary and secondary school</a>, <a href="/wiki/University_Centre_in_Svalbard" title="University Centre in Svalbard">university</a>, sports center with a swimming pool, library, culture center, cinema,<sup id="cite_ref-chp10_70-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chp10-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> bus transport, hotels, a bank,<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> and several museums.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The newspaper <i><a href="/wiki/Svalbardposten" title="Svalbardposten">Svalbardposten</a></i> is published weekly.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Very little mining activity remains at Longyearbyen; coal mines at <a href="/wiki/Sveagruva" title="Sveagruva">Sveagruva</a> and Luckerfjellet suspended operations in 2017 and were closed permanently in 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Ny-%C3%85lesund" title="Ny-Ålesund">Ny-Ålesund</a> is a permanent research settlement in the northwest of <a href="/wiki/Spitsbergen" title="Spitsbergen">Spitsbergen</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Northernmost_settlements" class="mw-redirect" title="Northernmost settlements">northernmost functional civilian settlement</a> in the world. Formerly a mining town, it is still a <a href="/wiki/Company_town" title="Company town">company town</a> operated by the Norwegian state-owned <a href="/wiki/Kings_Bay_(company)" title="Kings Bay (company)">Kings Bay</a> company. While some tourism to the outpost is permitted, Norwegian authorities limit access to minimize impact on scientific work.<sup id="cite_ref-chp10_70-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chp10-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Ny-Ålesund has a winter population of 35 and a summer population of 180.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Meteorological_Institute" title="Norwegian Meteorological Institute">Norwegian Meteorological Institute</a> has outposts at Bjørnøya and Hopen, with ten and four inhabitants, respectively. Both can also house temporary research staff.<sup id="cite_ref-chp10_70-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chp10-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Poland operates the <a href="/wiki/Polish_Polar_Station" class="mw-redirect" title="Polish Polar Station">Polish Polar Station</a> at <a href="/wiki/Hornsund" title="Hornsund">Hornsund</a>, with ten permanent residents.<sup id="cite_ref-chp10_70-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chp10-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Piramida_Svalbard_IMG_6283.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/81/Piramida_Svalbard_IMG_6283.JPG/220px-Piramida_Svalbard_IMG_6283.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/81/Piramida_Svalbard_IMG_6283.JPG/330px-Piramida_Svalbard_IMG_6283.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/81/Piramida_Svalbard_IMG_6283.JPG/440px-Piramida_Svalbard_IMG_6283.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3888" data-file-height="2592" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Piramida_Svalbard_IMG_6283.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The abandoned Soviet mining town of <a href="/wiki/Pyramiden" title="Pyramiden">Pyramiden</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Soviet mining settlement of <a href="/wiki/Pyramiden" title="Pyramiden">Pyramiden</a> was abandoned in 1998, leaving <a href="/wiki/Barentsburg" title="Barentsburg">Barentsburg</a> as the only permanently inhabited Russian settlement. It is a company town: all facilities are owned by Arktikugol, which operates a coal mine. In addition to the mining facilities, Arktikugol has opened a hotel and souvenir shop, catering to tourists taking day trips or hikes from Longyearbyen.<sup id="cite_ref-chp10_70-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-chp10-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The village features a school, library, sports center, community center, swimming pool, farm, and greenhouse. Pyramiden features similar facilities; both are built in typical post-World War II Soviet architectural and planning style and contain the world's two most northerly <a href="/wiki/List_of_statues_of_Lenin" class="mw-redirect" title="List of statues of Lenin">Lenin statues</a> and other <a href="/wiki/Socialist_realism" title="Socialist realism">socialist realist</a> art.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> As of 2013<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, a handful of workers are stationed in the largely abandoned Pyramiden to maintain local infrastructure and run its hotel, which has been re-opened to tourism.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:1973HDR_Longyearbyen.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/1973HDR_Longyearbyen.jpg/220px-1973HDR_Longyearbyen.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/1973HDR_Longyearbyen.jpg/330px-1973HDR_Longyearbyen.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/1973HDR_Longyearbyen.jpg/440px-1973HDR_Longyearbyen.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3451" data-file-height="5173" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:1973HDR_Longyearbyen.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Church" title="Svalbard Church">Svalbard Church</a></div></div></div>
<p>Most of the population is Christian and affiliated with the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Norway" title="Church of Norway">Church of Norway</a>. Catholics on the archipelago are pastorally served by the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Territorial_Prelature_of_Troms%C3%B8" title="Roman Catholic Territorial Prelature of Tromsø">Territorial Prelature of Tromsø</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Svalbard" title="Politics of Svalbard">Politics of Svalbard</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Visa_policy_of_Svalbard" title="Visa policy of Svalbard">Visa policy of Svalbard</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Spitzbergen_nordsyssel_hg.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Spitzbergen_nordsyssel_hg.jpg/220px-Spitzbergen_nordsyssel_hg.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="139" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Spitzbergen_nordsyssel_hg.jpg/330px-Spitzbergen_nordsyssel_hg.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Spitzbergen_nordsyssel_hg.jpg/440px-Spitzbergen_nordsyssel_hg.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2452" data-file-height="1548" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Spitzbergen_nordsyssel_hg.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>MS <i>Nordsyssel</i>, the <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_Svalbard" title="Governor of Svalbard">Governor</a>'s vessel, docked at <a href="/wiki/Ny-%C3%85lesund" title="Ny-Ålesund">Ny-Ålesund</a></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Treaty" title="Svalbard Treaty">Svalbard Treaty</a> of 1920 established full Norwegian sovereignty over the archipelago. The islands are, unlike the <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Antarctic_territories" class="mw-redirect" title="Norwegian Antarctic territories">Norwegian Antarctic territories</a>, a part of the Kingdom of Norway and not a <a href="/wiki/Dependent_territory" title="Dependent territory">dependency</a>. The treaty came into effect in 1925, following the Svalbard Act. All forty-eight signatory countries of the treaty have the right to conduct commercial activities on the archipelago without discrimination, although all activity is subject to Norwegian legislation. The treaty limits Norway's right to collect taxes to that of financing services on Svalbard.<sup id="cite_ref-treaty_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-treaty-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Therefore, Svalbard has a lower <a href="/wiki/Income_tax" title="Income tax">income tax</a> than mainland Norway, and there is no <a href="/wiki/Value_added_tax" class="mw-redirect" title="Value added tax">value added tax</a>. There is a separate budget for Svalbard to ensure compliance. Svalbard is a <a href="/wiki/Demilitarized_zone" title="Demilitarized zone">demilitarized zone</a>, as the treaty prohibits the establishment of military installations. Norwegian military activity is limited to fishery surveillance by the <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Coast_Guard" title="Norwegian Coast Guard">Norwegian Coast Guard</a> as the treaty requires Norway to protect the natural environment.<sup id="cite_ref-treaty_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-treaty-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Svalbard is not governed by Norway's policies on migration and does not issue visas or residence permits itself.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Higgins_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Higgins-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> Foreigners do not need a visa or work and residence permits from the Norwegian authorities to travel to Svalbard. However, foreign citizens with a visa requirement for the Schengen Area must have a Schengen visa when travelling to and from Svalbard via mainland Norway.<sup id="cite_ref-Entry_and_residence_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Entry_and_residence-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Svalbard Act established the institution of the Governor of Svalbard (<a href="/wiki/Norwegian_language" title="Norwegian language">Norwegian</a>: <i lang="no">Sysselmester</i>, formerly <span title="Norwegian-language text"><i lang="no">Sysselmannen</i></span>), who holds the responsibility as both <a href="/wiki/County_governor_(Norway)" title="County governor (Norway)">county governor</a> and <a href="/wiki/Chief_of_police" title="Chief of police">chief of police</a>, as well as holding other authority granted from the executive branch. Duties include <a href="/wiki/Environmental_policy" title="Environmental policy">environmental policy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Family_law" title="Family law">family law</a>, <a href="/wiki/Law_enforcement" title="Law enforcement">law enforcement</a>, <a href="/wiki/Search_and_rescue" title="Search and rescue">search and rescue</a>, tourism management, information services, contact with foreign settlements, and judge in some areas of maritime inquiries and judicial examinations—albeit never in the same cases as acting as police.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> Since 2021, <a href="/wiki/Lars_Fause" title="Lars Fause">Lars Fause</a> has been governor. The institution is subordinate to the <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Ministry_of_Justice_and_the_Police" class="mw-redirect" title="Norwegian Ministry of Justice and the Police">Ministry of Justice and the Police</a>, but reports to other ministries in matters within their portfolio.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Lenin_statue_in_Barentsburg,_Svalbard.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Lenin_statue_in_Barentsburg%2C_Svalbard.jpg/220px-Lenin_statue_in_Barentsburg%2C_Svalbard.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Lenin_statue_in_Barentsburg%2C_Svalbard.jpg/330px-Lenin_statue_in_Barentsburg%2C_Svalbard.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Lenin_statue_in_Barentsburg%2C_Svalbard.jpg/440px-Lenin_statue_in_Barentsburg%2C_Svalbard.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Lenin_statue_in_Barentsburg,_Svalbard.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/List_of_statues_of_Vladimir_Lenin" title="List of statues of Vladimir Lenin">Lenin statue</a> in <a href="/wiki/Barentsburg" title="Barentsburg">Barentsburg</a></div></div></div>
<p>Since 2002, <a href="/wiki/Longyearbyen_Community_Council" title="Longyearbyen Community Council">Longyearbyen Community Council</a> has had many of the same responsibilities of a <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_Norway" class="mw-redirect" title="Municipalities of Norway">municipality</a>, including utilities, education, cultural facilities, fire department, roads, and ports.<sup id="cite_ref-naering_76-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-naering-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> No care or nursing services are available, nor are welfare payments available. Norwegian residents retain pension and medical rights through their mainland municipalities.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> The hospital is part of <a href="/wiki/University_Hospital_of_North_Norway" title="University Hospital of North Norway">University Hospital of North Norway</a>, while the airport is operated by state-owned <a href="/wiki/Avinor" title="Avinor">Avinor</a>. Ny-Ålesund and Barentsburg remain <a href="/wiki/Company_town" title="Company town">company towns</a> with all infrastructure owned by Kings Bay and Arktikugol.<sup id="cite_ref-naering_76-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-naering-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Other public offices with presence on Svalbard are the <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Directorate_of_Mining" title="Norwegian Directorate of Mining">Norwegian Directorate of Mining</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Polar_Institute" title="Norwegian Polar Institute">Norwegian Polar Institute</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Tax_Administration" title="Norwegian Tax Administration">Norwegian Tax Administration</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Norway" title="Church of Norway">Church of Norway</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Svalbard is subordinate to <a href="/wiki/Nord-Troms_District_Court" title="Nord-Troms District Court">Nord-Troms District Court</a> and <a href="/wiki/H%C3%A5logaland_Court_of_Appeal" title="Hålogaland Court of Appeal">Hålogaland Court of Appeal</a>, both in <a href="/wiki/Troms%C3%B8" title="Tromsø">Tromsø</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although Norway is part of the <a href="/wiki/European_Economic_Area" title="European Economic Area">European Economic Area</a> (EEA) and the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Agreement" title="Schengen Agreement">Schengen Agreement</a>, Svalbard is not part of the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Area" title="Schengen Area">Schengen Area</a> or the EEA.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Non-EU and non-Nordic Svalbard residents do not need Schengen visas for Svalbard itself, but those travelling via mainland Norway require visas to pass through Norway. People without a source of income can be rejected by the governor.<sup id="cite_ref-residence_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-residence-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>No one is required to have a <a href="/wiki/Visa_policy_of_Svalbard" title="Visa policy of Svalbard">visa</a> or residence permit on Svalbard. Regardless of citizenship, persons can live and work in Svalbard indefinitely. The Svalbard Treaty grants treaty nationals equal <a href="/wiki/Right_of_abode" title="Right of abode">right of abode</a> as Norwegian nationals. So far, non-treaty nationals have been admitted visa-free as well. While there is no visa requirement, everyone must meet certain requirements in order to stay in Svalbard. These requirements are governed by a separate policy called “<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://lovdata.no/dokument/SF/forskrift/1995-02-03-96">Regulations relating to rejection and expulsion of persons from Svalbard</a>”. Among the requirements is that residents must have the means to be able to reside on Svalbard. These requirements apply to both foreigners and Norwegian citizens, and the Governor of Svalbard may reject persons who do not meet the requirements.<sup id="cite_ref-Entry_and_residence_89-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Entry_and_residence-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (June 2021)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Russia retains a <a href="/wiki/Diplomatic_missions_of_Russia" class="mw-redirect" title="Diplomatic missions of Russia">consulate in Barentsburg</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 2010, a treaty was made between Russia and Norway fixing the boundary between the Svalbard archipelago and the <a href="/wiki/Novaya_Zemlya_archipelago" class="mw-redirect" title="Novaya Zemlya archipelago">Novaya Zemlya archipelago</a>. Increased interest in petroleum exploration in the Arctic raised interest in a resolution of the dispute. The agreement takes into account the relative positions of the archipelagos, rather than being based simply on northward extension of the continental border of Norway and Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+style+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+link+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .ambox+.mw-empty-elt+link+.ambox,.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg/42px-Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg.png" decoding="async" width="42" height="34" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg/63px-Ambox_current_red_Americas.svg.png 1.5x, class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>.
