<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Svalbard" title="Economy of Svalbard">Economy of Svalbard</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ecotourism_Svalbard.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/Ecotourism_Svalbard.JPG/220px-Ecotourism_Svalbard.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/Ecotourism_Svalbard.JPG/330px-Ecotourism_Svalbard.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a0/Ecotourism_Svalbard.JPG/440px-Ecotourism_Svalbard.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3309" data-file-height="2240" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ecotourism_Svalbard.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tourists viewing a <a href="/wiki/Glacier" title="Glacier">glacier</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Svalbard_1987_British_schoolboys_camping_and_climbing.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/35/Svalbard_1987_British_schoolboys_camping_and_climbing.jpg/220px-Svalbard_1987_British_schoolboys_camping_and_climbing.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="150" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/35/Svalbard_1987_British_schoolboys_camping_and_climbing.jpg/330px-Svalbard_1987_British_schoolboys_camping_and_climbing.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/35/Svalbard_1987_British_schoolboys_camping_and_climbing.jpg/440px-Svalbard_1987_British_schoolboys_camping_and_climbing.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3056" data-file-height="2080" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Svalbard_1987_British_schoolboys_camping_and_climbing.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>British schoolboys camping, exploring and climbing, north-west Svalbard, 1987</div></div></div>
<p>The three main industries on Svalbard are <a href="/wiki/Coal_mining" title="Coal mining">coal mining</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tourism" title="Tourism">tourism</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Research" title="Research">research</a>. In 2007, there were 484 people working in the mining sector, 211 people working in the tourism sector, and 111 people working in the education sector. The same year, the mining yielded revenues of 2.008&#160;billion <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_krone" title="Norwegian krone">Norwegian kroner</a> (US$227,791,078), tourism 317&#160;million kroner (US$35,967,202), and research 142&#160;million kroner (US$16,098,404).<sup id="cite_ref-naering_76-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-naering-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2006, the average income for economically active people was 494,700 kroner, 23% higher than on the mainland.<sup id="cite_ref-focus_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-focus-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Almost all housing is owned by the various employers and institutions and rented to their employees; there are only a few privately owned houses, most of which are recreational cabins. Because of this, it is difficult to live on Svalbard without working for an established institution.<sup id="cite_ref-residence_97-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-residence-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since the resettlement of Svalbard in the early 20th century, coal mining has been the dominant commercial activity. <a href="/wiki/Store_Norske_Spitsbergen_Kulkompani" title="Store Norske Spitsbergen Kulkompani">Store Norske Spitsbergen Kulkompani</a>, a subsidiary of the <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Ministry_of_Trade_and_Industry" class="mw-redirect" title="Norwegian Ministry of Trade and Industry">Norwegian Ministry of Trade and Industry</a>, operates Svea Nord in Sveagruva and Mine 7 in Longyearbyen. The former produced 3.4&#160;million <a href="/wiki/Tonne" title="Tonne">tonnes</a> in 2008, while the latter uses 35% of its output to fuel the Longyearbyen Power Station. Since 2007, there has not been any significant mining by the Russian state-owned Arktikugol in Barentsburg.<sup id="cite_ref-naering_76-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-naering-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There has been test drilling for petroleum on land, but these did not give satisfactory results for permanent operation. Norwegian authorities do not allow offshore petroleum activities for environmental reasons, and the land formerly test-drilled have been protected as natural reserves or national parks.<sup id="cite_ref-naering_76-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-naering-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, a 20-year plan to develop offshore oil and gas resources around Svalbard was announced.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ny-Alesund_(js)_5.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Ny-Alesund_%28js%29_5.jpg/220px-Ny-Alesund_%28js%29_5.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Ny-Alesund_%28js%29_5.jpg/330px-Ny-Alesund_%28js%29_5.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Ny-Alesund_%28js%29_5.jpg/440px-Ny-Alesund_%28js%29_5.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5555" data-file-height="3644" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ny-Alesund_(js)_5.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/NASA" title="NASA">NASA</a> research facility in <a href="/wiki/Ny-%C3%85lesund" title="Ny-Ålesund">Ny-Ålesund</a></div></div></div>
<p>Svalbard has historically been a base for both <a href="/wiki/Whaling" title="Whaling">whaling</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fishing" title="Fishing">fishing</a>. Norway claimed a 200-nautical-mile (370-kilometre) <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> (EEZ) around Svalbard in 1977,<sup id="cite_ref-fisheries_14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fisheries-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> with 31,688 square kilometres (9,239 square nautical miles) of <a href="/wiki/Internal_waters" title="Internal waters">internal waters</a> and 770,565 square kilometres (224,661 square nautical miles) of EEZ.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> Norway retains a restrictive fisheries policy in the zone,<sup id="cite_ref-fisheries_14-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fisheries-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> and the claims are disputed by Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_10-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Tourism is focused on the environment and is centered on Longyearbyen. Activities include hiking, kayaking, walks through glacier caves, and <a href="/wiki/Snowmobile" title="Snowmobile">snowmobile</a> and dog-sled safari. Cruise ships generate a significant portion of the traffic, including both stops by offshore vessels and expeditionary cruises starting and ending in Svalbard. Traffic is strongly concentrated between March and August; overnight stays have quintupled from 1991 to 2008, when there were 93,000 overnight stays.<sup id="cite_ref-naering_76-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-naering-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Research on Svalbard centers on Longyearbyen and Ny-Ålesund, the most accessible areas in the high Arctic. The Svalbard Treaty grants permission for any nation to conduct research on Svalbard, resulting in the <a href="/wiki/Polish_Polar_Station" class="mw-redirect" title="Polish Polar Station">Polish Polar Station</a> and the Chinese <a href="/wiki/Arctic_Yellow_River_Station" class="mw-redirect" title="Arctic Yellow River Station">Arctic Yellow River Station</a>, plus Russian facilities in Barentsburg.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/University_Centre_in_Svalbard" title="University Centre in Svalbard">University Centre in Svalbard</a> in Longyearbyen offers undergraduate, graduate, and postgraduate courses to 350 students in various arctic sciences, particularly <a href="/wiki/Biology" title="Biology">biology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Geology" title="Geology">geology</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Geophysics" title="Geophysics">geophysics</a>. Courses are provided to supplement studies at mainland universities; there are no tuition fees and courses are held in English, with Norwegian and international students equally represented.<sup id="cite_ref-unis_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unis-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Global_Seed_Vault" title="Svalbard Global Seed Vault">Svalbard Global Seed Vault</a> is a <a href="/wiki/Seedbank" class="mw-redirect" title="Seedbank">seedbank</a> to store seeds from as many of the world's crop varieties and their botanical wild relatives as possible. A cooperation between the government of Norway and the <a href="/wiki/Global_Crop_Diversity_Trust" class="mw-redirect" title="Global Crop Diversity Trust">Global Crop Diversity Trust</a>, the vault is cut into rock near Longyearbyen, keeping it at a natural −6&#160;°C (21&#160;°F) and refrigerating the seeds to −18&#160;°C (0&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Undersea_Cable_System" title="Svalbard Undersea Cable System">Svalbard Undersea Cable System</a> is a 1,440&#160;km (890&#160;mi) <a href="/wiki/Optical_fiber" title="Optical fiber">fibre optic</a> line from Svalbard to <a href="/wiki/Harstad" title="Harstad">Harstad</a>, needed for communicating with <a href="/wiki/Polar_orbit" title="Polar orbit">polar orbiting</a> <a href="/wiki/Satellite" title="Satellite">satellites</a> through <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Satellite_Station" title="Svalbard Satellite Station">Svalbard Satellite Station</a> and installations in Ny-Ålesund.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Arctic_World_Archive" title="Arctic World Archive">Arctic World Archive</a>, a huge digital archiving concern run by Norwegian private company <a href="/w/index.php?title=Piql&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Piql (page does not exist)">Piql</a> and the state-owned coal-mining company <a href="/wiki/Store_Norske_Spitsbergen_Kulkompani" title="Store Norske Spitsbergen Kulkompani">Store Norske Spitsbergen Kulkompani</a>, opened in March 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-vincent_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vincent-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> In mid-2020, it acquired its biggest customer in the form of <a href="/wiki/GitHub" title="GitHub">GitHub</a>, a subsidiary of <a href="/wiki/Microsoft" title="Microsoft">Microsoft</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-lindner_113-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lindner-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>One source of income for the area was, until 2015, visiting cruise ships. The Norwegian government became concerned about large numbers of cruise ship passengers suddenly landing at small settlements such as Ny-Ålesund, which is conveniently close to the barren-yet-picturesque <a href="/wiki/Magdalenefjorden" title="Magdalenefjorden">Magdalena Fjord</a>. With the increasing size of the larger ships, up to 2,000 people can potentially appear in a community that normally numbers less than 40. As a result, the government severely restricted the size of cruise ships that may visit.<sup id="cite_ref-cruise-ship-ban_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cruise-ship-ban-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Unemployment" title="Unemployment">Unemployment</a> is effectively nonexistent as there is no <a href="/wiki/Welfare" title="Welfare">public assistance</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Higgins_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Higgins-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Svalbard" title="Transport in Svalbard">Transport in Svalbard</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Longyear01.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Longyear01.jpg/220px-Longyear01.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Longyear01.jpg/330px-Longyear01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7f/Longyear01.jpg/440px-Longyear01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Longyear01.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Snowmobile" title="Snowmobile">Snowmobiles</a> are an important mode of transport in Svalbard, such as here at Longyearbyen.</div></div></div>
<p>In Longyearbyen, Barentsburg, and Ny-Ålesund, there are road networks, but they do not connect with each other. <a href="/wiki/Off-road" class="mw-redirect" title="Off-road">Off-road</a> motorized transport is prohibited on bare ground in Svalbard, but snowmobiles are used extensively during winter—both for commercial and recreational activities. Transport from Longyearbyen to Barentsburg (45&#160;km or 28&#160;mi) and Pyramiden (100&#160;km or 62&#160;mi) is possible by snowmobile in winter, or by ship all year round. All settlements have ports and Longyearbyen has a bus system.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Template:Harvard_citation_documentation#Wikilink_to_citation_does_not_work" title="Template:Harvard citation documentation"><span title="Template:Harvard citation documentation#Wikilink to citation does not work (November 2022)">citation not found</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Svalbard_Airport,_Longyear" class="mw-redirect" title="Svalbard Airport, Longyear">Svalbard Airport, Longyear</a>, 3 kilometres (2&#160;mi) from Longyearbyen, is the only airport offering air transport off the archipelago. <a href="/wiki/Scandinavian_Airlines" title="Scandinavian Airlines">Scandinavian Airlines</a> has daily scheduled services to <a href="/wiki/Troms%C3%B8" title="Tromsø">Tromsø</a> and <a href="/wiki/Oslo" title="Oslo">Oslo</a>. Low-cost carrier <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Air_Shuttle" title="Norwegian Air Shuttle">Norwegian Air Shuttle</a> also has a service between Oslo and Svalbard, operating three or four times a week; there are also irregular charter services to Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Finnair" title="Finnair">Finnair</a> operated service from <a href="/wiki/Helsinki" title="Helsinki">Helsinki</a>, operating three times per week between June and August 2016, but Norwegian authorities disallowed this route, citing the 1978 bilateral agreement on air traffic between Finland and Norway.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Lufttransport" title="Lufttransport">Lufttransport</a> provides regular corporate charter services from Longyearbyen to <a href="/wiki/Ny-%C3%85lesund_Airport,_Hamnerabben" title="Ny-Ålesund Airport, Hamnerabben">Ny-Ålesund Airport, Hamnerabben</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Svea_Airport" title="Svea Airport">Svea Airport</a> for Kings Bay and Store Norske. These flights are generally not available to the public.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> There are <a href="/wiki/Heliport" title="Heliport">heliports</a> in Barentsburg and Pyramiden, and helicopters are frequently used by the governor and to a lesser extent the mining company Arktikugol.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Svalbard" title="Climate of Svalbard">Climate of Svalbard</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Spitzbergen-2_hg.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Spitzbergen-2_hg.jpg/220px-Spitzbergen-2_hg.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Spitzbergen-2_hg.jpg/330px-Spitzbergen-2_hg.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/95/Spitzbergen-2_hg.jpg/440px-Spitzbergen-2_hg.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1920" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Spitzbergen-2_hg.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Spitsbergen" title="Spitsbergen">Spitsbergen</a> in August</div></div></div>
<p>The climate of Svalbard is dominated by its high latitude, with the average daily mean summer temperature  at 4 to 7&#160;°C (39 to 45&#160;°F) (1991-2020 averages), and January averages at −13 to −9&#160;°C (9 to 16&#160;°F) (1991-2020). The more southern Bear Island has January mean temperatures as mild as −4.6&#160;°C (24&#160;°F) in the 1991-2020 base period.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/West_Spitsbergen_Current" title="West Spitsbergen Current">West Spitsbergen Current</a>, the northernmost branch of the <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic_Current" title="North Atlantic Current">North Atlantic Current</a> system, moderates Svalbard's temperatures, particularly during winter. Winter temperatures in Svalbard are up to 20&#160;°C (36&#160;°F) higher than those at similar latitudes in Russia and Canada. The warm Atlantic water keeps the surrounding waters open and navigable most of the year. The interior fjord areas and valleys, sheltered by the mountains, have larger temperature differences than the coast, giving about 2&#160;°C (4&#160;°F) warmer summer temperatures and 3&#160;°C (5&#160;°F) colder winter temperatures.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On the south of Spitsbergen, the temperature is slightly higher than further north and west. During winter, the temperature difference between south and north is typically 5&#160;°C (9&#160;°F), and about 3&#160;°C (5&#160;°F) in summer. <a href="/wiki/Bear_Island_(Norway)" title="Bear Island (Norway)">Bear Island</a> has average temperatures even higher than the rest of the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Svalbard is where cold <a href="/wiki/Polar_Air_Mass" class="mw-redirect" title="Polar Air Mass">polar air</a> from the north and mild, wet sea air from the south meet, creating low pressure, changeable weather and strong winds, particularly in winter; in January, a strong breeze is registered 17% of the time at <a href="/wiki/Isfjord_Radio" title="Isfjord Radio">Isfjord Radio</a>, but only 1% of the time in July. In summer, <a href="/wiki/Fog" title="Fog">fog</a> is common, particularly off the coast, with visibility under 1 kilometre (0.6&#160;mi) registered 20% of the time in July and 1% of the time in January, at Hopen and Bjørnøya.<sup id="cite_ref-t101_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-t101-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Precipitation is frequent, but falls in small quantities, typically less than 400 millimetres (16&#160;in) per year in western Spitsbergen. More rain falls on the uninhabited east side, where there can be more than 1,000 millimetres (39&#160;in).<sup id="cite_ref-t101_125-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-t101-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> On average, Svalbard has lower <a href="/wiki/Humidity" title="Humidity">humidity</a> than other places in the <a href="/wiki/Arctic_Circle" title="Arctic Circle">Arctic Circle</a>. The only places in the Arctic with a lower average are in mainland <a href="/wiki/Norway" title="Norway">Norway</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sweden" title="Sweden">Sweden</a> and <a href="/wiki/Finland" title="Finland">Finland</a>).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>2016 was the warmest year on record at Svalbard Airport, with a remarkable mean temperature of 0.0&#160;°C (32.0&#160;°F), 7.5&#160;°C (13.5&#160;°F) above the 1961–90 average, and more comparable to a location at the <a href="/wiki/Arctic_circle" class="mw-redirect" title="Arctic circle">arctic circle</a>. The coldest temperature of the year was as high as −18&#160;°C (0&#160;°F), warmer than the mean minimum in a normal January, February or March. In the same year, the number of days when there was rainfall equalled the number of days when there was snowfall, a significant deviation from the usual pattern whereby there would be at least twice as many snow days.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Global_warming" class="mw-redirect" title="Global warming">Global warming</a> has resulted in noticeable climatic changes on Svalbard. Between 1970 and 2020, the average temperature on Svalbard rose by 4 degrees Celsius, and in the winter months by 7 degrees.<sup id="cite_ref-HanssenBauer_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HanssenBauer-127">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> On 25 July 2020, a new record temperature of 21.7&#160;°C (71.1&#160;°F) was measured for the Svalbard archipelago, which is also the highest temperature ever recorded in the European part of the High Arctic; in addition, temperatures of over 20 degrees were measured four days in a row in July 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As in large parts of the Arctic, the <a href="/wiki/Ice%E2%80%93albedo_feedback" title="Ice–albedo feedback">ice–albedo feedback</a> effects can also be noticed on Svalbard: Due to the substantial ice melt, ice surfaces are transformed into open water, the darker surface of which absorbs more solar energy instead of reflecting it back; as a result, these waters heat up and further ice in the area melts faster and faster, creating more open waters, etc. A temperature increase of between 7 and 10 degrees is expected on Svalbard by the end of the century.<sup id="cite_ref-HanssenBauer_127-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HanssenBauer-127">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Nature">Nature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Nature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Environment_of_Svalbard" title="Environment of Svalbard">Environment of Svalbard</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Long-tailed_Skua_(js)_26.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Long-tailed_Skua_%28js%29_26.jpg/220px-Long-tailed_Skua_%28js%29_26.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Long-tailed_Skua_%28js%29_26.jpg/330px-Long-tailed_Skua_%28js%29_26.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4c/Long-tailed_Skua_%28js%29_26.jpg/440px-Long-tailed_Skua_%28js%29_26.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4869" data-file-height="3264" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Long-tailed_Skua_(js)_26.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/Long-tailed_skua" class="mw-redirect" title="Long-tailed skua">Long-tailed skua</a></div></div></div>
<p>In addition to humans, three primarily terrestrial mammalian species inhabit the archipelago: the <a href="/wiki/Arctic_fox" title="Arctic fox">Arctic fox</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_reindeer" title="Svalbard reindeer">Svalbard reindeer</a>, and accidentally introduced <a href="/wiki/Southern_vole" class="mw-redirect" title="Southern vole">southern voles</a>, which are found only in Grumant.<sup id="cite_ref-vernebrosjyre_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vernebrosjyre-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Attempts to introduce the <a href="/wiki/Arctic_hare" title="Arctic hare">Arctic hare</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Muskox" title="Muskox">muskox</a> have both failed.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> There are 15 to 20 types of <a href="/wiki/Marine_mammal" title="Marine mammal">marine mammals</a>, including: <a href="/wiki/Whale" title="Whale">whales</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dolphin" title="Dolphin">dolphins</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pinniped" title="Pinniped">seals</a>, <a href="/wiki/Walrus" title="Walrus">walruses</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Polar_bear" title="Polar bear">polar bears</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-vernebrosjyre_129-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vernebrosjyre-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Polar bears are the iconic symbol of Svalbard, and one of the main tourist attractions.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> The animals are protected and people moving outside the settlements are required to have appropriate scare devices to ward off attacks. They are also advised to carry a firearm for use as a last resort.<sup id="cite_ref-Umbreit_2005:_132_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Umbreit_2005:_132-132">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> A British schoolboy was killed by a polar bear in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> In July 2018, a polar bear was shot dead after it attacked and injured a polar bear guard leading tourists off a cruise ship.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> In August 2020 a <a href="/wiki/Netherlands" title="Netherlands">Dutch</a> man was killed by a polar bear at a campsite in Longyearbyen. The polar bear was shot dead.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> In 2022, a polar bear attacked a French tourist, who suffered injuries to an arm. The bear left after shots had been fired. It was later <a href="/wiki/Animal_euthanasia" title="Animal euthanasia">euthanised</a> following a professional assessment of its injuries.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2021, Svalbard has around 300 resident<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> polar bears. Svalbard and <a href="/wiki/Franz_Joseph_Land" class="mw-redirect" title="Franz Joseph Land">Franz Joseph Land</a> share a common population of 3,000 polar bears, with <a href="/wiki/Kong_Karls_Land" title="Kong Karls Land">Kong Karls Land</a> being the most important breeding ground.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Female_polar_bear_(Ursus_maritimus)_with_cub,_Svalbard.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Female_polar_bear_%28Ursus_maritimus%29_with_cub%2C_Svalbard.jpg/220px-Female_polar_bear_%28Ursus_maritimus%29_with_cub%2C_Svalbard.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Female_polar_bear_%28Ursus_maritimus%29_with_cub%2C_Svalbard.jpg/330px-Female_polar_bear_%28Ursus_maritimus%29_with_cub%2C_Svalbard.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/af/Female_polar_bear_%28Ursus_maritimus%29_with_cub%2C_Svalbard.jpg/440px-Female_polar_bear_%28Ursus_maritimus%29_with_cub%2C_Svalbard.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4041" data-file-height="2273" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Female_polar_bear_(Ursus_maritimus)_with_cub,_Svalbard.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A female <a href="/wiki/Polar_bear" title="Polar bear">polar bear</a> with cub</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_reindeer" title="Svalbard reindeer">Svalbard reindeer</a> (<i>R. tarandus platyrhynchus</i>) is a distinct subspecies; although it was previously almost extinct, it can be legally hunted (as can Arctic fox).<sup id="cite_ref-vernebrosjyre_129-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vernebrosjyre-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> There are limited numbers of domesticated animals in the Russian settlements.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tundra1_(js).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Tundra1_%28js%29.jpg/220px-Tundra1_%28js%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Tundra1_%28js%29.jpg/330px-Tundra1_%28js%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Tundra1_%28js%29.jpg/440px-Tundra1_%28js%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5560" data-file-height="3642" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tundra1_(js).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tundra at <a href="/wiki/Bellsund" title="Bellsund">Bellsund</a></div></div></div>
<p>About eighty species of bird are found on Svalbard, most of which are migratory.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Barents_Sea" title="Barents Sea">Barents Sea</a> is among the areas in the world with most seabirds, with about 20&#160;million individuals during late summer. The most common are: <a href="/wiki/Little_auk" title="Little auk">little auk</a>, <a href="/wiki/Northern_fulmar" title="Northern fulmar">northern fulmar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Thick-billed_murre" title="Thick-billed murre">thick-billed murre</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Black-legged_kittiwake" title="Black-legged kittiwake">black-legged kittiwake</a>. Sixteen species are on the <a href="/wiki/IUCN_Red_List" title="IUCN Red List">IUCN Red List</a>. Particularly Bjørnøya, <a href="/wiki/Storfjorden,_Svalbard" class="mw-redirect" title="Storfjorden, Svalbard">Storfjorden</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nordvest-Spitsbergen_Land_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="Nordvest-Spitsbergen Land National Park">Nordvest-Spitsbergen</a>, and Hopen are important <a href="/wiki/Breeding_ground" class="mw-redirect" title="Breeding ground">breeding ground</a> for seabirds. The <a href="/wiki/Arctic_tern" title="Arctic tern">Arctic tern</a> has the furthest migration, all the way to <a href="/wiki/Antarctica" title="Antarctica">Antarctica</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-vernebrosjyre_129-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vernebrosjyre-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Two songbirds migrate to Svalbard to breed: the <a href="/wiki/Snow_bunting" title="Snow bunting">snow bunting</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Northern_wheatear" title="Northern wheatear">northern wheatear</a>. <a href="/wiki/Rock_ptarmigan" title="Rock ptarmigan">Rock ptarmigan</a> is the only bird to overwinter.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> Remains of <a href="/wiki/Predator_X" class="mw-redirect" title="Predator X">Predator X</a> (<i>Pliosaurus funkei</i>) from the <a href="/wiki/Jurassic" title="Jurassic">Jurassic</a> period were discovered here. It is one of the largest dinosaur-era <a href="/wiki/Marine_reptile" title="Marine reptile">marine reptiles</a> ever found.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:B%C3%BCnsow_Land_Brisingefjellet_IMG_7496.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/B%C3%BCnsow_Land_Brisingefjellet_IMG_7496.JPG/220px-B%C3%BCnsow_Land_Brisingefjellet_IMG_7496.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/B%C3%BCnsow_Land_Brisingefjellet_IMG_7496.JPG/330px-B%C3%BCnsow_Land_Brisingefjellet_IMG_7496.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/B%C3%BCnsow_Land_Brisingefjellet_IMG_7496.JPG/440px-B%C3%BCnsow_Land_Brisingefjellet_IMG_7496.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:B%C3%BCnsow_Land_Brisingefjellet_IMG_7496.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>West coast of <a href="/wiki/B%C3%BCnsow_Land" title="Bünsow Land">Bünsow Land</a> at <a href="/wiki/Isfjorden_(Svalbard)" title="Isfjorden (Svalbard)">Isfjorden</a> in Spitsbergen</div></div></div>
<p>Svalbard has <a href="/wiki/Permafrost" title="Permafrost">permafrost</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tundra" title="Tundra">tundra</a>, including low, middle, and high <a href="/wiki/Arctic_vegetation" title="Arctic vegetation">Arctic vegetation</a>. One hundred and sixty-five species of plants have been found on the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-vernebrosjyre_129-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vernebrosjyre-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Only those areas which defrost in the summer are vegetated, which accounts for about 10% of the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> Vegetation is most abundant in Nordenskiöld Land, around Isfjorden and where affected by <a href="/wiki/Guano" title="Guano">guano</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> While there is little precipitation, giving the archipelago a <a href="/wiki/Steppe" title="Steppe">steppe</a> climate, plants still have good access to water because the cold climate reduces evaporation.<sup id="cite_ref-t101_125-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-t101-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-vernebrosjyre_129-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vernebrosjyre-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> The growing season is very short, and may last only a few weeks.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Svalbard_poppy" class="mw-redirect" title="Svalbard poppy">Svalbard poppy</a> (<i>Papaver dahlianum</i>) is the symbolic flower of Svalbard.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Prins-karls-forrland_pho.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Prins-karls-forrland_pho.jpg/220px-Prins-karls-forrland_pho.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="93" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Prins-karls-forrland_pho.jpg/330px-Prins-karls-forrland_pho.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Prins-karls-forrland_pho.jpg/440px-Prins-karls-forrland_pho.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="434" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Prins-karls-forrland_pho.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Prins_Karls_Forland" title="Prins Karls Forland">Prins Karls Forland</a> was protected as <a href="/wiki/Forlandet_National_Park" title="Forlandet National Park">Forlandet National Park</a> in 1973</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Total_solar_eclipse_of_March_20,_2015_by_Damien_Deltenre_(licensed_for_free_use)._(32844461616).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Total_solar_eclipse_of_March_20%2C_2015_by_Damien_Deltenre_%28licensed_for_free_use%29._%2832844461616%29.jpg/220px-Total_solar_eclipse_of_March_20%2C_2015_by_Damien_Deltenre_%28licensed_for_free_use%29._%2832844461616%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Total_solar_eclipse_of_March_20%2C_2015_by_Damien_Deltenre_%28licensed_for_free_use%29._%2832844461616%29.jpg/330px-Total_solar_eclipse_of_March_20%2C_2015_by_Damien_Deltenre_%28licensed_for_free_use%29._%2832844461616%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bd/Total_solar_eclipse_of_March_20%2C_2015_by_Damien_Deltenre_%28licensed_for_free_use%29._%2832844461616%29.jpg/440px-Total_solar_eclipse_of_March_20%2C_2015_by_Damien_Deltenre_%28licensed_for_free_use%29._%2832844461616%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="600" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Total_solar_eclipse_of_March_20,_2015_by_Damien_Deltenre_(licensed_for_free_use)._(32844461616).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Total solar eclipse of 20 March 2015, Longyearbyen</div></div></div>
<p>There are <a href="/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_Norway" title="List of national parks of Norway">seven national parks</a> in Svalbard: <a href="/wiki/Forlandet_National_Park" title="Forlandet National Park">Forlandet</a>, <a href="/wiki/Indre_Wijdefjorden_National_Park" title="Indre Wijdefjorden National Park">Indre Wijdefjorden</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nordenski%C3%B6ld_Land_National_Park" title="Nordenskiöld Land National Park">Nordenskiöld Land</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nordre_Isfjorden_National_Park" title="Nordre Isfjorden National Park">Nordre Isfjorden Land</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nordvest-Spitsbergen_National_Park" title="Nordvest-Spitsbergen National Park">Nordvest-Spitsbergen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sassen-B%C3%BCnsow_Land_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="Sassen-Bünsow Land National Park">Sassen-Bünsow Land</a> and <a href="/wiki/S%C3%B8r-Spitsbergen_National_Park" title="Sør-Spitsbergen National Park">Sør-Spitsbergen</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The archipelago has fifteen bird sanctuaries, one geotopic protected area and six nature reserves—with <a href="/wiki/Nordaust-Svalbard_Nature_Reserve" title="Nordaust-Svalbard Nature Reserve">Nordaust-Svalbard</a> and <a href="/wiki/S%C3%B8raust-Svalbard_Nature_Reserve" title="Søraust-Svalbard Nature Reserve">Søraust-Svalbard</a> both being larger than any of the national parks. Most of the nature reserves and three of the national parks were created in 1973, with the remaining areas gaining protection in the 2000s.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> All human traces dating from before 1946 are automatically protected.<sup id="cite_ref-Umbreit_2005:_132_132-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Umbreit_2005:_132-132">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> The protected areas make up 65% of the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-focus_104-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-focus-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> Svalbard is on Norway's tentative list for nomination as a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The total <a href="/wiki/Solar_eclipse_of_March_20,_2015" title="Solar eclipse of March 20, 2015">solar eclipse of 20 March 2015</a> included only Svalbard and the <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands" title="Faroe Islands">Faroe Islands</a> in the band of totality.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Academic_grading_in_Svalbard" title="Academic grading in Svalbard">Academic grading in Svalbard</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Longyearbyen_School" title="Longyearbyen School">Longyearbyen School</a> serves ages 6–18. It is the primary/secondary school in the <a href="/wiki/List_of_northernmost_items" title="List of northernmost items">northernmost location on Earth</a>. Once pupils reach ages 16 or 17, most families move to mainland Norway.<sup id="cite_ref-freezer_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-freezer-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Barentsburg" title="Barentsburg">Barentsburg</a> has its own school serving the Russian community; by 2014 it had three teachers, and its welfare funds had declined.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> A primary school served the community of <a href="/wiki/Pyramiden" title="Pyramiden">Pyramiden</a> in the pre-1998 period.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is a non-degree offering <a href="/wiki/Tertiary_education" title="Tertiary education">tertiary educational</a> institution in Longyearbyen,<sup id="cite_ref-freezer_151-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-freezer-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/University_Centre_in_Svalbard" title="University Centre in Svalbard">University Centre in Svalbard</a> (UNIS), the northernmost tertiary school on Earth.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul class="gallery mw-gallery-packed">
		<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 178.66666666667px"><div style="width: 178.66666666667px">

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Svalbard&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Association football</a> is the most popular sport in Svalbard. There are three football pitches (one at Barentsburg), but no stadiums because of the small population.<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> There is also an indoor hall adopted for multiple sports including <a href="/wiki/Futsal" title="Futsal">indoor football</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
